## Related Work

Parametric knowledge and retrieval. Language models <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ore</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>assoc</sub>i<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r parame</sub>t<sub>ers,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t <sub>reca</sub>ll <sub>an</sub>d u<sub>p</sub>datin<sub>g</sub> remain unreliable (Petroni et al. 2019; Roberts, Rafel, and Shazeer 2020). Retrieval-au<sub>g</sub>mented s<sub>y</sub>stems externalize document memor<sub>y</sub> throu<sub>g</sub>h a retriever (Kar<sub>p</sub>ukhin et al. 2020; Lewis et al. 2020), while knowled<sub>g</sub>e editin<sub>g</sub> tar-<sub>g</sub>ets localized factual chan<sub>g</sub>es (Mitchell et al. 2022; Men<sub>g</sub> et al. 2022; Wan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2024; Cohen et al. 2024). Document knowledge acquisition. ## A Unified Objective View

L<sub>e</sub>t ${ \mathcal { D } } _ { \mathrm { d o c } } = \{ d _ { i } \}$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t d<sub>ocumen</sub>t<sub>s an</sub>d $\mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { Q A } } =$ $\left\{ \left( q _ { i } , a _ { i } \right) \right\}$ denote document-derived QA pairs. Each Inject objective m constructs a recipe dataset $\mathcal { D } _ { m } ~ = ~ \{ ( u , y ) \}$ where u is an instruction with an optional document prefix or compressed representation and y is the supervised document tar<sub>g</sub>et. <sup>L</sup>et $n _ { m }$ b<sub>e</sub> it<sub>s rea</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>row coun</sub>t <sub>a</sub>ft<sub>er m</sub>i<sub>x</sub>t<sub>ure sam-</sub> <sub>p</sub>li<sub>ng</sub> <sub>an</sub>d l<sub>eng</sub>th filt<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d l<sub>e</sub>t $\textstyle \pi _ { m } = n _ { m } / \sum _ { k } n _ { k }$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> Inject objective is

$$
\begin{array} { r l } { \mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { i n j } } } & { = \displaystyle \sum _ { m \in \mathcal { M } } \pi _ { m } \mathrm { E } _ { ( u , y ) \sim \mathcal { D } _ { m } } [ \ell _ { \theta } ( u , y ) ] , } \\ { \ell _ { \theta } ( u , y ) } & { = \displaystyle - \frac { 1 } { | y | } \sum _ { t = 1 } ^ { | y | } \log p _ { \theta } ( y _ { t } \mid u , y _ { < t } ) . } \end{array}\tag{1}
$$

This difers <sub>opera</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>raw</sub> <sub>con</sub>ti<sub>nue</sub>d <sub>pre</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng:</sub>

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { C P T } } = - \frac { 1 } { T } \sum _ { t = 1 } ^ { T } \log p _ { \theta } ( x _ { t } \mid x _ { < t } ) .\tag{2}

## Stage 1: Inject

The Inject stage uses three document-generation objectives and their mixtures. Continuation predicts a document sufix from an instruction-conditioned prefix. Rewrite reconstructs th<sub>e c</sub>l<sub>eane</sub>d d<sub>ocumen</sub>t f<sub>rom a genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>summary, ou</sub>tli<sub>ne,</sub> or knowledge skeleton. Instruction-formatted reconstruction <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e c</sub>l<sub>eane</sub>d d<sub>ocumen</sub>t f<sub>rom a s</sub>h<sub>or</sub>t <sub>gener</sub>i<sub>c rea</sub>d<sub>-</sub> ing instruction. These objectives provide denser supervised document tar<sub>g</sub>ets than QA-onl<sub>y</sub> trainin<sub>g</sub> without usin<sub>g</sub> raw-<sub>s</sub>tr<sub>ea</sub>m CPT l<sub>oss.</sub> S<sub>upp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>m<sub>e</sub>nt<sub>a</sub>r<sub>y</sub> M<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>ri<sub>a</sub>l<sub>,</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n B <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>exac</sub>t i<sub>npu</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> <sub>mas</sub>k<sub>s,</sub> <sub>m</sub>i<sub>x</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> <sub>no</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>promp</sub>t <sub>sc</sub>h<sub>e</sub>m<sub>as;</sub> S<sub>upp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>m<sub>e</sub>nt<sub>a</sub>r<sub>y</sub> M<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>ri<sub>a</sub>l<sub>,</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n C <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> tr<sub>a</sub>inin<sub>g</sub> h<sub>yperparame</sub>t<sub>ers an</sub>d <sub>rea</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>row coun</sub>t<sub>s.</sub>

## Stage 2: Align

The Align stage fine-tunes the injected model on domain QA pairs. For a question x and answer y, we use answer-only <sub>superv</sub>i<sub>se</sub>d fi<sub>ne-</sub>t<sub>un</sub>i<sub>ng:</sub>

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { a l i g n } } = - \frac { 1 } { \left| a \right| } \sum _ { t = 1 } ^ { \left| a \right| } \log p _ { \theta } ( a _ { t } \mid q , a _ { < t } ) .\tag{3}
$$

V<sub>an</sub>ill<sub>a</sub> SFT <sub>op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>zes</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> l<sub>oss</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l $\theta _ { 0 } ,$ , while IAR optimizes it from the injected check-<sub>p</sub>o<sup>i</sup>nt $\theta _ { I } \mathbf { : }$ :

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \theta _ { \mathrm { S F T } } = \mathrm { A l i g n } ( \theta _ { 0 } ) , \qquad \theta _ { \mathrm { I A } } = \mathrm { A l i g n } ( \theta _ { I } ) . } \end{array}\tag{4}
$$

We use IA to denote this pre-recovery Inject+Align checkpoint. We ask four questions: RQ1, whether IAR improves <sub>re</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>eva</sub>l<sub>-</sub>f<sub>ree</sub> d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>n-genera</sub>l <sub>opera</sub>ti<sub>ng po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s over</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>t SFT and conventional CPT+SFT; RQ2, whether extended QA-only training explains the gains; RQ3, whether Inject+Align improves domain internalization before Recover; and RQ4, whether the recovery pattern persists across larger Qwen3 models on CC. The CC-onl<sub>y</sub> SDFT, LoRA, Repla<sub>y</sub>, and FAPM stress test is <sub>g</sub>rouped under RQ1, while Qwen <sub>sca</sub>li<sub>ng rema</sub>i<sub>ns separa</sub>t<sub>e</sub> f<sub>rom cross-</sub>f<sub>am</sub>il<sub>y compar</sub>i<sub>sons.</sub>

## Evaluation

Domain QA is evaluated with an adaptive LLM panel. Two judges score each answer; a third judge is queried only when th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t t<sub>wo scores</sub> dif<sub>er, an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>or</sub>di<sub>na</sub>l <sub>score</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> median. Per-judge outputs are stored before aggregation. The archived <sub>p</sub>anel confi<sub>g</sub>urations use gpt-oss-120b (O<sub>p</sub>enAI 2025), MiniMax-M2.5 (MiniMaxAI 2025), and Dee<sub>p</sub>Seek-V3 variants (Dee<sub>p</sub>Seek-AI 2025). Across 242,255 <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>-answer</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ances,</sub> th<sub>e au</sub>dit fi<sub>n</sub>d<sub>s</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t<sub>-</sub>t<sub>wo</sub> <sub>exac</sub>t <sub>ag</sub>r<sub>ee</sub>m<sub>e</sub>nt <sub>o</sub>f <sub>.</sub>707<sub>,</sub> bin<sub>a</sub>r<sub>y ag</sub>r<sub>ee</sub>m<sub>e</sub>nt <sub>o</sub>f <sub>.</sub>848 <sub>a</sub>ft<sub>e</sub>r <sub>co</sub>l<sub>-</sub> <sup>l</sup>a<sub>p</sub>s<sup>i</sup>n<sub>g</sub> scores at $\geq . 5 ,$ bi<sub>nary</sub> C<sub>o</sub>h<sub>en</sub>’<sub>s</sub> $\kappa ~ = ~ . 6 9 1$ <sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> third-judge trigger rate of .297. LLM-as-judge evaluation is <sub>use</sub>f<sub>u</sub>l b<sub>u</sub>t i<sub>mper</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>, so we</sub> t<sub>rea</sub>t it <sub>as an au</sub>dit<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>rumen</sub>t rather than a <sub>g</sub>old label source (Zhen<sub>g</sub> et al. 2023). ## A Dataset and Task Examples

CC and CCI are document-derived QA evaluations. CC is derived from Common Cor<sub>p</sub>us (PleIAs 2024); CCI is derived from the CCI dataset (BAAI 2024). In both cases, trainin<sub>g</sub> <sub>examp</sub>l<sub>es are genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom source</sub> d<sub>ocumen</sub>t<sub>s an</sub>d t<sub>es</sub>t <sub>ex-</sub> <sub>amp</sub>l<sub>es are</sub> h<sub>e</sub>ld <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>.</sub> At i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> ti<sub>me, a</sub>ll <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> <sub>rece</sub>i<sub>ve on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e ques</sub>ti<sub>on, no</sub>t th<sub>e source</sub> d<sub>ocumen</sub>t <sub>or a re-</sub> t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>eve</sub>d <sub>passage.</sub> Thi<sub>s ma</sub>k<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k d<sub>e</sub>lib<sub>era</sub>t<sub>e</sub>l<sub>y s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>t<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> RAG-st<sub>y</sub>le document QA. T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 4<sub>:</sub> D<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on con</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>/t<sub>es</sub>t <sub>sp</sub>lit <sub>an</sub>d <sub>re</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>eva</sub>l<sub>-</sub>f<sub>ree</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t <sub>co</sub>l<sub>umn</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub> th<sub>e exper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t<sub>a</sub>l <sub>se</sub>tti<sub>ng: mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> must answer from internalized <sub>p</sub>arameters rather than from retrieved source <sub>p</sub>assa<sub>g</sub>es. <table><tr><td>Step</td><td>Prompt schema</td><td>Output contract</td></tr><tr><td>Anchor extraction</td><td>Given a text chunk, extract at most K independently referable core objects. JSON list of anchors. Prefer explicit concepts, methods, mechanisms, modules, devices, or technical terms appearing in the text. Do not output deictic objects such as “this method&quot; or “the above mechanism.&quot;</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Type applicability</td><td>Given a question-type description and the text chunk, decide whether the chunk yes/no. can support a question of that type. The supported types are factual extraction, mechanism explanation, design rationale, condition/constraint, limitation/trade-</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Question generation</td><td>off, and comparison/relation. Judge prompt contract. The domain and MSBench judges share the same evidence inputs, but use diferent outpu<sup>t and a</sup>gg<sup>re</sup>g<sup>ation r</sup>u<sup>les:</sup>

Input: question, reference answer, model answer, optional <sub>scor</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>ru</sub>b<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c.</sub>

Decision: domain QA returns a score in {0, .5, 1}; MSBench <sub>re</sub>t<sub>urns</sub> bi<sub>nary</sub> <sub>correc</sub>t<sub>ness</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> <sub>qua</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> <sub>score.</sub>

Required rationale: short explanation identifying whether th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>answer con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> th<sub>e requ</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d f<sub>ac</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d <sub>w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> it i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uces</sub> <sub>unsuppor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Domain aggregation: two judges score every answer; if their scores difer, a third judge is called. The final score is the <sub>me</sub>di<sub>an o</sub>f th<sub>e ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e scores, an</sub>d <sub>every raw vo</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s s</sub>t<sub>ore</sub>d <sup>b</sup>e<sup>f</sup>ore a<sub>gg</sub>re<sub>g</sub>at<sup>i</sup>on.

MSBench also calls two judges first and a third on binary disagreement; final correctness uses majority vote and qualit<sub>y</sub> <sub>uses</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>mean</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>qua</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> <sub>scores.</sub> Th<sub>ese</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>are par</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d b<sub>ecause</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs are</sub> judge-produced rather than answer-key labels.

Judge agreement audit. The audit reads stored perexample V2 JSONL files without calling a model or judge. We use test for those files throughout the paper to avoid implying that fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>scores were a</sub>l<sub>so use</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> R<sub>ecover-can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Formal selection rule. For each dataset–model setting, let c denote a Recover candidate and let v denote the corre-<sub>spon</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> V<sub>an</sub>ill<sub>a</sub> SFT <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>sp</sub>lit<sub>.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> <sub>wr</sub>it<sub>e</sub> $D ( c )$ for retrieval-free domain QA accurac<sub>y</sub>, $I ( c )$ f<sub>or</sub> IFE<sub>va</sub>l<sub>,</sub> $\dot { M } ( c )$ f<sub>or</sub> MMLU<sub>,</sub> $B ( c )$ f<sub>or</sub> MSB<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>, an</sub>d

$$
G ( c ) = { \frac { I ( c ) + M ( c ) + B ( c ) } { 3 } }
$$

f<sub>or mean genera</sub>l <sub>per</sub>f<sub>ormance.</sub> W<sub>e use a</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d t<sub>o</sub>l<sub>erance o</sub>f $\tau = 1 . 0$ <sub>percen</sub>t<sub>age po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>.</sub> C<sub>an</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on procee</sub>d<sub>s as</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ows:</sub>

1. Domain feasibility. Keep candidates with $D ( c ) \geq$ $D ( v ) - \tau$ <sub>.</sub> Thi<sub>s a</sub>ll<sub>ows</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ary</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>d<sub>e-o</sub>f<sub>s suc</sub>h <sub>as</sub> Phi CCI <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>n</sub> l<sub>oss re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>o</sub> V<sub>an</sub>ill<sub>a</sub> SFT i<sub>s</sub> <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n</sub> t<sub>o</sub>l<sub>erance.</sub>

2. General guardrail. Among domain-feasible candidates, k<sub>eep can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es w</sub>ith $G ( c ) ~ \geq ~ G ( v )$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>t l<sub>eas</sub>t t<sub>wo</sub> <sub>o</sub>f $\{ I , M , B \}$ no more than τ below the corresponding V<sub>an</sub>ill<sub>a</sub> SFT <sub>va</sub>l<sub>ue.</sub>

3. Domain-primary ranking. Select from the nond<sub>om</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> $( D , G )$ <sub>p</sub>l<sub>ane.</sub> D<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>n ac-</sub> curac is the primar ke ; candidates within τ of the best <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>n score are</sub> t<sub>rea</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub> th<sub>e same</sub> d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>n</sub> ti<sub>er.</sub>

4. Tie-break. Within the best domain tier, choose the candidate with the largest G(c).
